3.3 Site & Battery Asset Management 
3.3.1 Manage Sites 
Function trigger: Web → Infrastructure → Sites. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Admin, Manager 
● Purpose: Maintain the installation locations that battery assets belong to. 
● Data processing: Persists the site with its address, GPS coordinates and installed capacity. The 
coordinates are what lets the platform pull weather data for the site, so they are required rather 
83 
 
than optional. Site detail aggregates the assets installed there and the latest environmental 
readings. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Name is required and unique per customer. Latitude and longitude must be 
valid coordinates. Installed capacity must be a positive number. 
● Normal case: The user creates or edits a site → saves → the site appears in the list and its detail 
screen shows its assets and environmental readings. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The coordinates are missing or invalid → the form refuses to save and explains that weather 
data cannot be resolved without them. 
○ The site still has assets attached and deletion is attempted → the action is refused until the 
assets are reassigned or retired. 
● Business rules: BR-08, BR-10 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Sites, Site Detail 
 
Figure 3.22 – Sites 
84 
 
 
Figure 3.23 – Site Detail 
3.3.2 Manage Battery Types and Thresholds 
Function trigger: Web → Infrastructure → Battery Types & Thresholds. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Admin 
● Purpose: Describe each battery model and the limits at which its readings become alerts. 
● Data processing: A battery type carries nominal capacity, nominal voltage, chemistry and rated 
cycle life. Thresholds are versioned: saving a change creates a new threshold version and 
activates it, leaving the previous version intact for historical interpretation of past alerts. Exactly 
one version is active per type at any moment. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Minimum values must be below their corresponding maximum. 
State-of-health and state-of-charge limits must lie between 0 and 100 percent. A type in use 
cannot be deleted. 
● Normal case: Admin edits the thresholds of a type in the dialog over the list → saves → a new 
version is created and activated → subsequent readings are evaluated against the new version 
while past alerts still reference the version that raised them. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ A minimum is set above its maximum → the form refuses to save. 
○ Admin tries to delete a type that assets still reference → the action is refused and the 
referencing assets are listed. 
● Business rules: BR-10, BR-11 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery Types & Thresholds 
85 
 
 
Figure 3.24 – Battery Types & Thresholds 
3.3.3 Manage Battery Assets 
Function trigger: Web → Infrastructure → Battery Assets. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Admin 
● Purpose: Register each physical battery pack and keep its placement current. 
● Data processing: An asset is identified by its serial number, which is unique platform-wide and 
immutable after registration. Each asset belongs to exactly one site and exactly one battery type; 
the type determines which threshold version applies to its readings. Retiring an asset stops alert 
evaluation without deleting its telemetry history. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Serial number is required and must be unique. Site and battery type are 
required. Installation date cannot be in the future. 
● Normal case: Admin registers an asset → it appears in the list and on its site → incoming 
readings tagged with that serial number are evaluated against the active threshold version of its 
type. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The serial number already exists → MSG-ERR-11 is shown. 
○ Admin attempts to change the serial number of an existing asset → the field is read-only. 
○ The asset is retired while it still has an open alert → the alert is closed with an explanatory 
system note. 
● Business rules: BR-09, BR-10, BR-11 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery Assets, Battery Asset Detail 
86 
 
 
Figure 3.25 – Battery Assets 
 
Figure 3.26 – Battery Asset Detail 
3.3.4 Browse Own Battery Assets 
Function trigger: Mobile → Dashboard → Battery List, or Profile → the listed asset. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer 
● Purpose: Let a customer see the condition of the packs they own. 
87 
 
● Data processing: Returns only the assets owned by the authenticated customer, with the latest 
reading, the cached state-of-health estimate and any open alert. Asset detail streams live 
readings and offers navigation to the site, to the related alert and to raising a ticket about the 
asset. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: The customer may only address assets they own; a request for another 
customer's asset is refused by the server regardless of what the client sends. 
● Normal case: The customer opens the list → taps an asset → the detail screen streams live 
readings, shows history charts and the current health estimate, and links to the site, the related 
alerts and the create-ticket form. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The customer owns no assets yet → an empty state explains that assets are registered by the 
operator. 
○ The asset has not reported recently → the screen shows the age of the last reading rather 
than presenting stale data as current. 
○ The asset id does not belong to the customer → MSG-ERR-08 is returned. 
● Business rules: BR-08 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery List, Battery Detail, Site Detail

3.3.5 Monitor Telemetry in Real Time 
Function trigger: Any dashboard or asset detail screen, on web or mobile. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer, Staff, Manager, Admin 
89 
 
● Purpose: Present the fleet as it is now rather than as it was at the last refresh. 
● Data processing: Readings accepted by the ingestion endpoint are fanned out over a persistent 
connection to every client currently displaying the affected asset or its site. The client merges the 
new point into the open chart without refetching the series. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A client only receives readings for assets its role and ownership allow it to 
see. 
● Normal case: The user opens a dashboard or an asset → the current series is loaded once → 
subsequent readings arrive by push and the chart advances without user action. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The connection drops → the client reconnects and backfills the gap from the history 
endpoint. 
○ The gateway is offline → the screen shows the timestamp of the last reading and marks the 
asset as not reporting. 
● Business rules: BR-13 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Dashboard, Battery Asset Detail, Site Detail

3.4 Telemetry Ingestion & IoT Device Management 
3.4.1 Ingest Sensor Readings 
Function trigger: An IoT gateway posts a batch of readings to the ingestion endpoint. 
Function description:  
91 
 
● Actor: IoT Gateway 
● Purpose: Bring measurements from the battery management system into the platform reliably. 
● Data processing: The gateway authenticates with its own API key. The batch carries an 
idempotency key; a batch whose key has already been seen is acknowledged and discarded 
rather than stored twice. Accepted readings are appended to the TimescaleDB hypertable, 
fanned out to connected clients, and evaluated against the active threshold version for each 
asset. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: The API key must be valid and not revoked. Every reading must carry a known 
asset serial number, a timestamp and at least one measured value. Timestamps far in the future 
are rejected. 
● Normal case: The gateway posts a batch → the key is verified → unseen readings are appended, 
streamed and evaluated → the gateway receives an acknowledgement and clears its local buffer. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The API key is invalid or revoked → MSG-ERR-16 is returned and nothing is stored. 
○ The batch repeats an idempotency key already processed → it is acknowledged without 
being stored again, so a gateway can safely resend after a connectivity loss. 
○ A reading references an unknown serial number → that reading is rejected and reported 
back while the rest of the batch is accepted. 
○ The gateway has been offline → it replays its buffer on reconnect and the replay is 
deduplicated by the same mechanism. 
● Business rules: BR-13, BR-14 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: — (machine-to-machine) 
3.4.2 Report Device Heartbeat 
Function trigger: The gateway posts its own health telemetry every 60 seconds. 
Function description:  
● Actor: IoT Gateway 
● Purpose: Let the platform tell a silent battery apart from a failed gateway. 
● Data processing: Stores CPU, memory, disk, queue depth and signal strength per device. A device 
whose heartbeat stops for longer than the configured window is marked offline and raises a 
device alert, so an absence of readings is never mistaken for a healthy quiet pack. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: The heartbeat must carry a valid device API key and a monotonic timestamp. 
● Normal case: The gateway reports every 60 seconds → the device stays marked online and its 
health metrics are visible on the device detail screen. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The heartbeat stops → after the configured window the device is marked offline and 
MSG-WRN-04 is raised. 
○ Queue depth grows persistently → the device detail screen surfaces it as a symptom of a 
gateway that cannot drain its buffer. 
92 
 
● Business rules: BR-33 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: IoT Device Detail 
 
Figure 3.33 – IoT Device Detail 
3.4.3 Manage IoT Devices 
Function trigger: Web → Infrastructure → IoT Devices. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Admin 
● Purpose: Maintain the fleet of edge devices and their credentials. 
● Data processing: Registering a device issues an API key shown once. The key can be rotated 
independently per device, so a compromised gateway can be cut off without touching the rest of 
the fleet. The list shows online or offline state derived from the heartbeat stream. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Device name and site are required. A device must be attached to exactly one 
site. 
● Normal case: Admin registers a device → copies the issued API key into the gateway 
configuration → the device begins reporting and appears online in the list. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ Admin rotates the key while the device is still using the old one → the device is refused with 
MSG-ERR-16 until its configuration is updated. 
○ Admin deletes a device that has reported readings → the device record is retired rather than 
removed, so historical readings keep their provenance. 
● Business rules: BR-32 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: IoT Devices, Add Device, IoT Device Detail, Edit Device 
93 
 
 
Figure 3.34 – IoT Devices 
 
Figure 3.35 – Add Device 
94 
 
 
Figure 3.36 – IoT Device Detail 
 
Figure 3.37 – Edit Device 
3.4.4 Record Device Calibration 
Function trigger: Web → Device Calibration → "Record calibration"; Mobile → Tools hub → 
Calibration → Create. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Staff 
95 
 
● Purpose: Keep sensor accuracy verifiable and prevent silent measurement drift. 
● Data processing: Stores the calibration offset and scale factor applied to a device's sensors, 
together with the technician, the timestamp and the expiry date. The platform lists calibrations 
approaching expiry so they can be scheduled before accuracy lapses. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Offset and scale factor are required numbers. The expiry date must be later 
than the calibration date. 
● Normal case: The technician records the calibration on site → it is stored against the device → 
the device leaves the expiring list and its next expiry date is set. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The expiry date is not after the calibration date → the form refuses to save. 
○ A calibration is due to expire within the warning window → MSG-WRN-05 is raised to 
Manager and Staff. 
● Business rules: BR-34 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Device Calibration, Create Calibration (Mobile) 
 
Figure 3.38 – Device Calibration 
96 
 
 
Figure 3.39 – Create Calibration (Mobile) 
3.5 Alerts & Environmental Incidents 
3.5.1 Generate Threshold Alert 
Function trigger: Automatically, whenever an accepted reading is evaluated. 
Function description:  
● Actor: System Scheduler 
● Purpose: Turn a measurement that has left its safe band into something a human will act on. 
● Data processing: Each reading is compared against the active threshold version for its asset's 
battery type. A breach opens an alert carrying the anomaly type, the measured value and the 
threshold that was crossed. Repeats of the same anomaly on the same asset are merged into the 
existing open alert rather than creating one alert per reading, and short-lived boundary crossings 
are recorded as noise breach events so a value oscillating around a limit does not produce a 
storm. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Only assets in service are evaluated; retired assets and assets covered by an 
active silence rule are skipped. 
● Normal case: A reading breaches a threshold → an alert is opened and pushed to the customer 
and to the internal operators → subsequent breaching readings of the same type update the 
same alert. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The same anomaly is already open on that asset → the existing alert is updated instead of a 
new one being created. 
97 
 
○ A silence rule covers the asset, its site or its type → the breach is recorded as silenced and 
no notification is sent. 
○ Five or more assets at one site raise the same anomaly inside the aggregation window → the 
individual alerts are merged into a single site alert. 
● Business rules: BR-11, BR-15, BR-16, BR-18 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery Alerts, Battery Asset Detail 
 
Figure 3.40 – Battery Alerts 
 
98 
 
Figure 3.41 – Battery Asset Detail 
3.5.2 Acknowledge and Resolve an Alert 
Function trigger: Web → Battery Alerts → open an alert; Mobile → Alerts → Alert Detail. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Record that a human has taken ownership of an alert and later that it has been dealt 
with. 
● Data processing: Acknowledging stops the escalation ladder and assigns ownership. Resolving 
closes the alert with an outcome note. Every state change is appended to the alert history with 
the actor, the timestamp and the reason, so the trail is reconstructable after the fact. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A resolution requires an outcome note. Only an acknowledged alert may be 
resolved. 
● Normal case: An operator opens the alert → acknowledges it, which stops escalation → 
performs the work, usually through the linked ticket → resolves the alert with a note → the alert 
leaves the open list and its history records both transitions. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The alert has already been acknowledged by someone else → the screen shows the current 
owner and the action is not offered again. 
○ The operator tries to resolve without acknowledging → the transition is refused. 
○ The underlying condition reappears after resolution → a new alert is opened rather than the 
closed one being reused. 
● Business rules: BR-17, BR-36 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery Alerts, Alert Detail 
99 
 
 
Figure 3.42 – Battery Alerts 
 
Figure 3.43 – Alert Detail 
3.5.3 Escalate an Unacknowledged Alert 
Function trigger: Automatically, on the acknowledgement timer. 
Function description:  
● Actor: System Scheduler 
100 
 
● Purpose: Guarantee that no alert can sit unowned. 
● Data processing: An alert that has not been acknowledged escalates on a fixed ladder: at 15 
minutes it is escalated to Staff, and at 30 minutes to Manager, at which point a P1 ticket is 
opened automatically and linked to the alert. Each step is written to the acknowledgement 
timeline. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: The ladder applies only to alerts that are still open and not covered by a 
silence rule. 
● Normal case: The alert is raised → nobody acknowledges it → at 15 minutes Staff are notified → 
at 30 minutes Manager is notified and a P1 ticket is created and linked → the ticket's SLA timer 
starts. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The alert is acknowledged before a step falls due → the ladder stops and no further 
escalation occurs. 
○ A ticket already exists for the alert → the escalation links to it instead of opening a second 
ticket. 
● Business rules: BR-17, BR-20, BR-21 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery Alerts, Ticket Detail 
 
Figure 3.44 – Battery Alerts 
101 
 
 
Figure 3.45 – Ticket Detail 
3.5.4 Silence Alerts Temporarily 
Function trigger: Web → Battery Alerts → "Silence". 
Function description:  
● Actor: Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Stop known noise from an issue that is already being worked on. 
● Data processing: A silence rule is scoped to an asset, a site or a battery type and carries a validity 
window and a reason. While it is in force, matching breaches are recorded as silenced rather 
than delivered, so the underlying data is still complete when the rule expires. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A scope, an expiry time and a reason are all required. A rule cannot be 
open-ended. 
● Normal case: The manager creates a rule for the asset under repair → matching alerts stop 
being delivered → the rule expires at its stated time and alerting resumes automatically. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The rule expires while the condition is still present → a fresh alert is raised immediately. 
○ A rule already covers the requested scope → the existing rule is shown for extension instead 
of a duplicate being created. 
● Business rules: BR-18 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery Alerts 
102 
 
 
Figure 3.46 – Battery Alerts 
3.5.5 Record an Environmental Incident 
Function trigger: Web → Environmental Incidents → "New incident"; also raised automatically from 
ambient breaches. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Staff, Manager, Admin, System Scheduler 
● Purpose: Capture a safety event that is more serious than a threshold breach. 
● Data processing: An incident records what was observed — smoke, water ingress, abnormal 
ambient temperature or humidity — at which site, with photographs and a severity. An incident 
may spawn an alert and a ticket, and unlike an alert it can never be closed automatically. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Site, incident type, severity and description are required. At least one 
photograph is expected for a physically observed incident. 
● Normal case: The operator records the incident → notifications go out on the configured 
channels → a ticket is opened for the response → the incident is closed manually with an 
outcome once the response is complete. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ Ambient readings breach the site's configured limits → an incident is raised automatically 
and linked to the readings that triggered it. 
○ An operator attempts to close an incident with no outcome recorded → the action is 
refused. 
● Business rules: BR-19 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Environmental Incidents, Incident Detail 
103 
 
 
Figure 3.47 – Environmental Incidents 
 
Figure 3.48 – Incident Detail 
3.5.6 Configure Ambient Thresholds 
Function trigger: Web → Infrastructure → Ambient Config. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Admin, Manager 
● Purpose: Set the environmental limits of a site, which are distinct from pack temperature limits. 
104 
 
● Data processing: Stores per-site limits for ambient temperature and humidity. Ambient readings 
arrive either from an environmental sensor at the site or from a weather API resolved through 
the site's GPS coordinates, and are evaluated against these limits. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Minimum must be below maximum for each measure. A site must have 
coordinates before weather data can be used as a source. 
● Normal case: The user sets the limits for a site → saves → ambient readings for that site are 
evaluated against the new limits. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The site has no coordinates → the screen explains that only sensor-sourced ambient data 
will be evaluated. 
○ A minimum is set above its maximum → the form refuses to save. 
● Business rules: BR-19 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Ambient Config 
 
Figure 3.49 – Ambient Config 
3.6 AI Health Prediction — as Consumed by the Clients 
3.6.1 Display Predicted State of Health 
Function trigger: Automatically whenever a battery asset detail screen or a fleet dashboard is 
opened; the value itself is refreshed on a background schedule, not by the screen. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer, Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Show how much usable life a pack has left, before the symptoms are visible in raw 
readings. 
105 
 
● Data processing: The platform sends a window of recent readings to the AI module on a 
schedule and caches what comes back — the state-of-health percentage, a confidence score, the 
model version and the measured latency — against the asset. Screens read the cached value 
only, so opening a screen never triggers an inference. This specification covers the fields the 
clients render; the model architecture and training pipeline belong to the AI module's own 
documentation. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A prediction requires a minimum amount of recent history. Below that the 
asset is reported as having insufficient history rather than being given a low-confidence guess. 
Customers see only their own assets. 
● Normal case: The user opens asset detail or a dashboard → the cached health percentage is 
rendered with its confidence, its model version and the time it was produced → the fleet view 
aggregates the same values into a health distribution. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The estimate falls below 80 percent → MSG-WRN-03 is raised and the pack is flagged for 
replacement planning. 
○ The AI module could not be reached at the last refresh → the last cached value is shown 
together with its age, MSG-ERR-19 is recorded, and threshold-based alerting continues 
unaffected. 
○ The asset has too little history → the screen states that a prediction is not yet available 
instead of showing a placeholder number. 
○ The confidence is low → it is displayed as such rather than hidden, so the operator can weigh 
it against the threshold evaluation. 
● Business rules: BR-12 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery Asset Detail, Dashboard, Analytics 
 
106 
 
Figure 3.50 – Battery Asset Detail 
 
Figure 3.51 – Dashboard 
 
Figure 3.52 – Analytics 
3.6.2 Display Anomaly Classification 
Function trigger: Alongside the health value on asset detail, and on any alert or ticket raised from a 
classification. 
Function description:  
107 
 
● Actor: Customer, Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Separate a pack that is merely operating near a limit from one that is genuinely 
misbehaving. 
● Data processing: The cached classification is one of Normal, Degrading or Failed, stored with its 
confidence and model version. When it reports Degrading or Failed the platform opens a ticket 
automatically at the priority implied by the severity. A technician can record feedback against a 
classification; the feedback is retained for later model improvement and never overwrites the 
stored result. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: The label is always rendered as text, never by colour alone. Feedback may 
only be submitted by an internal user and must state whether the classification was correct. 
● Normal case: The classifier reports Degrading or Failed → a ticket is opened automatically and 
linked to the asset → the technician sees the classification on the ticket and confirms or corrects 
it through feedback while resolving. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The classification contradicts the threshold evaluation → both are shown and the more 
severe of the two drives the response. 
○ The AI module could not be reached → the last cached classification is shown with its age 
and no ticket is opened on AI grounds alone; a threshold breach still opens one on its own 
merits. 
○ A technician marks the classification wrong → the feedback is stored against it and the 
displayed result is unchanged. 
● Business rules: BR-12, BR-20 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery Asset Detail, Battery Alerts, Ticket Detail 
 
108 
 
Figure 3.53 – Battery Asset Detail 
 
Figure 3.54 – Battery Alerts 
 
Figure 3.55 – Ticket Detail 
109 
 
3.7 Ticket Management & SLA 
3.7.1 Create a Support Ticket 
Function trigger: Mobile → Tickets tab → "Create ticket"; or Battery Detail → "Report a problem". 
A ticket may also be raised without any human action — see the three origins below. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer 
● Purpose: Let a customer ask for help and have the request tracked to a deadline. 
● Data processing: A ticket records where it came from in TicketOriginEnum. ManualByCustomer 
(1) is raised by the customer from the mobile app against one battery asset. AutoFromAlert (2) 
is raised by the system when a battery alert is severe enough, also against one asset. System 
(4) is raised from a cascade-risk assessment or an environmental incident and may cover a 
whole site, in which case no single asset is attached. Whatever the origin, the ticket enters the 
queue in state Open with the description, any photographs and the link to the asset 
concerned; the SLA clock does not run yet. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A title and description are required. Attachments must be within the size and 
type limits. The linked asset, when supplied, must belong to the customer. 
● Normal case: The customer fills the form and submits → the ticket is created in Open and 
appears in the manager queue → the create screen is replaced by the ticket detail so the 
customer can follow progress. An auto-raised ticket reaches the same queue without anyone 
filling a form. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ An attachment exceeds the size limit → MSG-ERR-17 is shown and the ticket is not 
submitted until it is removed. 
○ The linked asset does not belong to the customer → MSG-ERR-08 is returned. 
○ The network drops mid-submission → the draft is preserved locally so the customer does not 
lose the description. 
● Business rules: BR-08, BR-23 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Create Ticket, Ticket Detail 
110 
 
 
Figure 3.56 – Create Ticket 
 
Figure 3.57 – Ticket Detail 
3.7.2 Triage and Assign a Ticket 
Function trigger: Web → Support → Ticket Queue → open a queued ticket. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Manager 
111 
 
● Purpose: Give every ticket a priority and an owner before its clock matters. 
● Data processing: The queue shares the ticket table with the ticket list, so opening a row leads to 
the same ticket detail. At intake the manager records the impact scope and the urgency; the 
priority — P1, P2 or P3 — is derived from them through the ITIL impact-versus-urgency matrix 
rather than typed in by hand, which is what keeps it consistent between managers. The derived 
priority fixes the resolution deadline at 14, 3 or 2 working days and starts the SLA timer. 
Assignment matches a technician by skill tier and current workload. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Priority, primary handler and a scheduled start time are all required to 
assign a ticket. The handler must meet the minimum skill tier for the priority: P3Normal needs 
Generalist or above, P2High needs ModuleSpecialist or above, P1Critical and Urgent need 
SeniorSpecialist. Supporting staff are optional and are not counted against their workload. 
● Normal case: The manager opens a queued ticket → picks the priority, the primary handler 
and a start time → a start time inside the last five minutes moves the ticket straight to 
InProgress and starts the SLA clock, while a future start time moves it to Pending (Scheduled) 
and a background worker activates it when the time arrives. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ A start time earlier than five minutes ago → the request is refused (400) and the manager 
must choose a new time. 
○ The request is outside the scope of the service → the manager rejects it while it is still Open 
and the ticket moves to ClosedRejected with a mandatory reason. 
○ No technician of the required tier is available → the ticket stays in the queue and is surfaced 
as blocked rather than being silently assigned. 
● Business rules: BR-20, BR-21, BR-24 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Ticket Queue (Manager), Ticket Detail (Manager), Assign Staff 
 
112 
 
Figure 3.58 – Ticket Queue (Manager) 
 
Figure 3.59 – Ticket Detail (Manager) 
 
Figure 3.60 – Assign Staff 
3.7.3 Process an Assigned Ticket 
Function trigger: Web → My Tickets → open a ticket; Mobile → Dashboard → open a ticket. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Staff 
113 
 
● Purpose: Move a ticket through its lifecycle and record what was actually done. 
● Data processing: The primary handler works the ticket while it is InProgress and records what 
was done in a maintenance log. Reporting the work finished moves the ticket to Completed 
and stops the SLA clock; the manager then either approves it to Closed or sends it back to 
InProgress. The handler may put the ticket on hold, which moves it to Pending (Held) with a 
reason and a new scheduled start, or request an escalation, which moves it to Request for the 
manager to decide. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Reporting completion requires a resolution summary. Only the primary 
handler may start, hold, complete or escalate the ticket; supporting staff may read and 
comment but cannot change its state. 
● Normal case: The technician opens the assigned ticket → it is InProgress once the scheduled 
time arrives → the work is performed and recorded in the maintenance log → reporting 
completion moves it to Completed and notifies the manager for approval. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ A transition that the lifecycle does not allow is attempted → MSG-ERR-14 is returned and 
nothing changes. 
○ The technician needs help → requesting an escalation moves the ticket to Request; when the 
manager approves it, the ticket moves to ReAssign to be given to a handler of a higher tier. 
○ The technician tries to report completion without a resolution summary → the action is 
refused. 
● Business rules: BR-23, BR-29 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Ticket Detail (Staff), Maintenance History 
 
Figure 3.61 – Ticket Detail (Staff) 
114 
 
 
Figure 3.62 – Maintenance History 
3.7.4 Track and Enforce the SLA 
Function trigger: Automatically, from the moment the ticket enters InProgress. 
Function description:  
● Actor: System Scheduler 
● Purpose: Make the resolution deadline real rather than aspirational. 
● Data processing: A countdown runs against the deadline implied by the priority and is displayed 
live on the ticket detail screen. Periods during which the ticket is legitimately blocked are 
recorded as pause events, and the countdown pauses automatically while the ticket waits on 
information from the customer, resuming as soon as they reply. As the deadline approaches, a 
warning is raised; on breach the ticket escalates to a higher technician tier and the original 
deadline is kept rather than extended. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Only a ticket with a priority has a timer. A pause requires a reason. 
● Normal case: The timer starts at intake → a warning is raised as the deadline approaches → if 
the ticket is resolved in time the timer stops → otherwise the ticket escalates to a higher tier 
while the deadline stays where it was. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The deadline passes → MSG-WRN-02 is raised, the ticket escalates and the breach is 
recorded against the SLA report. 
○ The ticket is legitimately waiting on the customer → the manager pauses the timer with a 
reason and the pause is recorded. 
○ The ticket is reopened later → a new timer is started rather than the old one resumed. 
● Business rules: BR-21, BR-22, BR-26 (see section 5.1). 
115 
 
● Screen layout: Ticket Detail (Staff), SLA Monitor 
 
Figure 3.63 – Ticket Detail (Staff) 
 
Figure 3.64 – SLA Monitor 
3.7.5 Rate and Reopen a Ticket 
Function trigger: Mobile → Ticket Detail, after the ticket reaches Closed. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer 
116 
 
● Purpose: Close the loop with the customer and give them recourse if the fix did not hold. 
● Data processing: Rating is available only to the customer who raised the ticket and only after 
resolution; submitting it moves the ticket to CLOSED. Reopening a closed ticket requires a stated 
reason, returns it to OPEN and starts a new SLA timer rather than resuming the old one. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A rating is required to close. A reopen reason is required and is written to the 
activity timeline. 
● Normal case: The customer receives the resolution notification → opens the ticket → submits a 
rating → the ticket moves to CLOSED. If the problem recurs, the customer reopens it with a 
reason and the workflow restarts at OPEN. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ Someone other than the raising customer attempts to rate → MSG-ERR-15 is returned. 
○ A rating is attempted before resolution → MSG-ERR-15 is returned. 
○ A reopen is submitted without a reason → the action is refused. 
● Business rules: BR-25, BR-26 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Ticket Detail 
 
Figure 3.65 – Ticket Detail 
3.7.6 Preventive Maintenance Scheduling 
Function trigger: Automatically, when a maintenance schedule falls due. 
Function description:  
● Actor: System Scheduler 
● Purpose: Do the work before the failure rather than after it. 
117 
 
● Data processing: A maintenance schedule attached to an asset defines a recurring task — 
cleaning, inspection, calibration — and its interval. When a schedule falls due the platform raises 
a ticket at standard priority, linked to the asset. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A schedule requires an asset, a task type and an interval. 
● Normal case: The schedule falls due → a P3 ticket is opened automatically and enters the queue 
→ the manager assigns it like any other ticket → completing it advances the schedule to its next 
due date. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ A ticket for the same schedule is still open → a second one is not created. 
○ The asset has been retired → the schedule is deactivated instead of producing tickets for 
equipment that no longer exists. 
● Business rules: BR-21, BR-23 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Ticket Queue (Manager), Ticket Detail (Manager) 
 
Figure 3.66 – Ticket Queue (Manager) 
118 
 
 
Figure 3.67 – Ticket Detail (Manager) 
3.7.7 Periodic Maintenance Cycle 
Function trigger: Automatically, when a battery reaches its next maintenance due date; and Mobile 
→ Ticket Detail → choose a visit time, for the Customer. 
Function description: 
Actor: System Scheduler, Customer, Manager 
Purpose: Keep a battery on a regular service rhythm once it has been serviced, and let the Customer 
say when the visit suits them instead of having a date imposed. 
Data processing: The maintenance schedule belongs to the battery asset: each asset carries its next 
due date, and the cycle length comes from its battery type, so different chemistries can be serviced 
on different rhythms. When the due date is reached, BatteryService records the cycle together with 
the battery's health at that moment and TicketService raises a maintenance ticket. The new ticket 
records the due date and the deadline by which the visit must be chosen; a ticket is periodic precisely 
because it carries a maintenance due date, and it is overdue once the due date has passed. Only one 
ticket may exist for the same battery and due date, so a restart of the worker does not raise 
duplicates. While the ticket waits for an answer, reminders go out at 08:00 local time: the first on the 
day it is raised, the second the day after, and on the third day the ticket is handed to the Manager to 
arrange instead. When the Customer picks a time, the ticket keeps its scheduled start and everyone 
following it is told the schedule changed. 
Function detail: 
Data & Validation: Only the Customer who owns the ticket may choose the time, and only while the 
ticket is still periodic, still Open and not yet assigned. The chosen time may not be in the past and 
may not fall after the scheduling deadline, which is seven days wide. Once that window closes the 
choice is refused and the Manager arranges the visit instead. 
119 
 
Normal case: A battery reaches its next maintenance due date → the cycle is recorded with the 
battery's health and a periodic ticket is raised for that battery → the Customer is reminded and picks 
a visit time within the window → the ticket carries that time into triage → the manager assigns it 
and it proceeds like any other maintenance ticket. 
Abnormal case: 
Someone other than the owning Customer tries to choose the time → the request is refused and 
nothing is changed. 
The ticket is not a periodic-maintenance ticket, or it has already been assigned → the scheduling 
action is rejected as not applicable. 
The chosen time is in the past, or later than the deadline → the request is refused and the Customer 
must pick another time. 
The Customer does not answer → after the second reminder the ticket is escalated to the Manager 
on the third day, and the visit is arranged without them. 
A periodic ticket for the same battery and due date already exists → a second one is not raised. 
Business rules: BR-43, BR-44 (see section 5.1). 
Screen layout: Ticket Detail 
120 
 
 
Figure 3.68 – Ticket Detail — periodic maintenance 
3.8 Ticket Chat 
3.8.1 Exchange Messages on a Ticket 
Function trigger: Ticket Detail → the chat panel, on web or mobile. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer, Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Give everyone involved in a ticket one place to talk, tied to the record itself. 
● Data processing: Messages are delivered in real time to every participant with an open thread. 
Each message carries its author, timestamp and read state per participant. A typing indicator 
shows when another participant is composing. Unread counts are maintained per user per ticket 
so a thread awaiting a reply is visible from the ticket list. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Only participants of the ticket may post. Message content is required unless 
an attachment is present. 
121 
 
● Normal case: A participant types and sends → the message appears immediately for every open 
thread → read receipts propagate as the other participants view it → the unread count clears for 
that user. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The recipient is offline → the message is stored and delivered when they next open the 
thread, and a push notification is sent according to their preferences. 
○ The ticket is closed → the thread becomes read-only for everyone except Admin. 
○ The connection drops mid-send → the client retries and the message is not duplicated. 
● Business rules: BR-27, BR-28 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Ticket Detail, Chat Inbox 
 
Figure 3.69 – Ticket Detail 
122 
 
 
Figure 3.70 – Chat Inbox 
3.8.2 Separate Public and Internal Threads 
Function trigger: Ticket Detail → thread switch (internal users only). 
Function description:  
● Actor: Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Let the team discuss a ticket without the customer reading it. 
● Data processing: The same ticket carries two threads. The public thread is visible to the 
customer; the internal thread is visible only to internal roles and is never returned to a customer 
session regardless of what the client requests. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: The thread switch is only rendered for internal roles; the server enforces the 
same rule independently. 
● Normal case: An internal user switches to the internal thread → posts a note → the customer's 
view of the ticket is unchanged and shows only the public thread. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ A customer session requests the internal thread → MSG-ERR-08 is returned. 
○ An internal user posts to the wrong thread → the message can be deleted by Admin, and the 
deletion is audit-logged. 
● Business rules: BR-27, BR-28 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Ticket Detail 
123 
 
 
Figure 3.71 – Ticket Detail 
3.8.3 Enrich a Conversation 
Function trigger: Ticket Detail → the chat composer and message context menu. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer, Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Make the conversation usable across languages, devices and file types. 
● Data processing: Participants can attach files and images, react with an emoji, mention 
another participant by typing “@” in the composer — which opens a picker listing the people 
on the ticket — translate a message into a target language, and ask the AI assistant for a 
suggested reply. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Attachments must satisfy the size and type limits. A mention must target a 
participant of the ticket. 
● Normal case: A participant attaches a photograph and mentions the technician → the technician 
receives a mention notification → they translate the customer's message into their own 
language → they request an AI draft, edit it and send. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The attachment exceeds the limit → MSG-ERR-17 is shown. 
○ The file type is not supported → MSG-ERR-18 is shown. 
○ The AI assistant is unavailable → the composer stays usable and only the suggestion button 
is disabled. 
● Business rules: BR-28 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Ticket Detail 
124 
 
 
Figure 3.72 – Ticket Detail 
3.8.4 Browse the Chat Inbox 
Function trigger: Mobile → Tools hub → Chat Inbox (Staff), Settings hub → Chat Inbox (Customer). 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer, Staff 
● Purpose: Give a user one list of every conversation they are part of, across tickets. 
● Data processing: Lists every thread the user participates in, ordered by most recent activity, with 
the unread count per thread. Opening an entry goes to the ticket detail with the chat panel 
focused. A separate Mentions view lists the messages that named the user. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A user only sees threads of tickets they participate in. 
● Normal case: The user opens the inbox → sees threads ordered by activity with unread badges 
→ taps one → lands on the ticket detail with the conversation open. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The user participates in no threads → an empty state is shown. 
○ A ticket the user participated in was closed → the thread remains listed and is read-only. 
● Business rules: BR-08, BR-27 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Chat Inbox, Ticket Detail

3.9 Guide & Blog 
3.9.1 Manage Guide Articles 
Function trigger: Web → Support → Guide → "New article" or open an article → Edit. 
Function description:  
126 
 
● Actor: Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Build a reusable body of technical guidance instead of rediscovering fixes per ticket. 
● Data processing: Articles are versioned: publishing an edit creates a new version rather than 
overwriting the previous one, so the guidance a technician followed on a past ticket stays 
recoverable. An article may be started from a template, and an open article can seed a new 
article from the template it uses. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Title and body are required. A published article must have at least one 
category. 
● Normal case: An internal user drafts a post, optionally starting from an existing one → 
publishes it → it appears in the blog list on web and mobile for the roles allowed to read it. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ Two users edit the same article concurrently → the second save is rejected and the newer 
version is offered for comparison. 
○ An article is referenced by a ticket and deletion is attempted → the article is unpublished 
rather than deleted so the reference stays resolvable. 
● Business rules: BR-30 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Guide, Create Guide Article, Guide Article Detail, Edit Guide Article 
 
Figure 3.75 – Guide 
127 
 
 
Figure 3.76 – Create Guide Article

3.9.2 Read the Guide 
Function trigger: Web → Guide; Mobile → Guide (Staff, from the Tools hub) or through a ticket. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer, Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Get the right guidance to whoever is facing the problem, including on site. 
● Data processing: The list is searchable and filterable by category. Article detail suggests related 
articles and lists the most popular ones. Ticket detail links into article detail through its related 
articles panel, which is the main path a technician takes to the Guide while working. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Customers see only articles published for customer visibility. 
● Normal case: A technician opens a ticket → follows a related article link → reads the procedure 
on their phone while on site → returns to the ticket. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ No article matches the search → an empty state suggests broadening the filter. 
○ An article is unpublished while being read → the reader is told the article is no longer 
available. 
● Business rules: BR-08, BR-30 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Guide, Guide Article Detail, Ticket Detail — Guide tab (Mobile)

3.9.3 Manage Blog Posts 
Function trigger: Web → Support → Blog. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Publish news and announcements to customers and to the field team. 
● Data processing: Blog posts are versioned in the same way as guide articles and carry a 
publication state. Any existing post can be reused as the skeleton for a new one, so the 
platform keeps no separate template catalogue. Manager and Admin may publish; Staff drafts 
are reviewed before they go live. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Title and body are required to publish. The slug must be unique among 
posts. 
● Normal case: An internal user drafts a post, optionally starting from an existing one → 
publishes it → it appears in the blog list on web and mobile for the roles allowed to read it. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ A Manager or Staff user attempts to edit a post they are not allowed to change → the action 
is not offered and the server refuses it (MSG-ERR-08). 
○ A published post is edited → a new version is created and the previous version stays in the 
history. 
● Business rules: BR-30, BR-31 (see section 5.1).

3.10 Notifications 
3.10.1 Deliver Notifications 
Function trigger: Automatically, whenever a notifiable event occurs. 
Function description:  
133 
 
● Actor: System Scheduler 
● Purpose: Get the right event to the right person on the channel they actually watch. 
● Data processing: Content is rendered from a stored template so wording stays consistent across 
channels. Each notification is routed to push, email and SMS according to the recipient's 
preferences and the channels currently enabled. Push targets the device tokens registered for 
that account; SMS is used only when the gateway is enabled. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A recipient must have at least one enabled channel for a given event type to 
receive it. Delivery failures are retried according to the channel policy. 
● Normal case: An event occurs — an alert is raised, a ticket changes state, a message arrives → 
the notification is rendered and dispatched on the recipient's enabled channels → opening it 
from the feed navigates directly to the source ticket or alert. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The device token has been invalidated by the platform → it is unregistered and the 
notification falls back to the next enabled channel. 
○ The SMS gateway is disabled or unreachable → the SMS channel is skipped and the failure is 
recorded without blocking the other channels. 
○ The recipient has disabled every channel for that event type → the notification is still written 
to their in-app feed. 
● Business rules: BR-36 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Notification feed, Notification Preferences 
 
Figure 3.86 – Notification feed 
134 
 
 
Figure 3.87 – Notification Preferences 
3.10.2 Manage Notification Preferences and Devices 
Function trigger: Web → Account Settings → Notifications; Mobile → Settings hub → Notification 
Preferences. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer, Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Let each user decide what interrupts them and where. 
● Data processing: Preferences are stored per channel and per event type. The devices section lists 
the mobile devices registered for push and allows any of them to be removed. Registration itself 
happens automatically after a successful mobile sign-in as a best-effort step that never blocks 
sign-in. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: At least the in-app channel remains enabled for safety-critical event types. 
● Normal case: The user opens preferences → turns off a channel for an event type → saves → 
subsequent notifications of that type skip that channel for that user. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The user attempts to disable every channel for a safety-critical type → the in-app channel 
stays enabled and the reason is explained. 
○ A device is removed → push notifications to it stop immediately. 
● Business rules: BR-36 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Account Settings — Notifications, Notification Preferences, Notification devices 
135 
 
 
Figure 3.88 – Account Settings — Notifications 
 
Figure 3.89 – Notification Preferences 
136 
 
 
Figure 3.90 – Notification devices 
3.10.3 Send a Broadcast Notification 
Function trigger: Web → System → Send Notification. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Admin 
● Purpose: Reach a group of users with an announcement outside the event-driven flow. 
● Data processing: Admin composes the message, selects the recipients by role or by explicit 
selection, chooses the channels and sends. Delivery follows each recipient's preferences within 
the chosen channels, and the result is reported as a count of successful and failed deliveries. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A title, a body and at least one recipient group are required. 
● Normal case: Admin composes and selects recipients → sends → MSG-INF-26 reports how many 
users were reached → recipients see the notification in their feed and on their enabled channels. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ A recipient has disabled the chosen channel → they still receive the in-app notification. 
○ No recipient matches the selection → the send is refused with an explanation. 
● Business rules: BR-36 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Send Notification 
137 
 
 
Figure 3.91 – Send Notification 
3.11 Energy & Fleet Analytics 
3.11.1 Derive Energy Sessions and Cycles 
Function trigger: Automatically, as readings arrive. 
Function description:  
● Actor: System Scheduler 
● Purpose: Turn a raw measurement stream into figures the business understands. 
● Data processing: Continuous charge or discharge intervals are grouped into energy sessions 
carrying the energy moved, the start and end state of charge and the peak power. A completed 
charge-discharge pair closes a cycle log with its depth of discharge, round-trip efficiency and a 
stress score, which is fed back as an input to the health model. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A session is only closed once the direction of current has reversed or the pack 
has been idle for the configured settling period. 
● Normal case: Readings arrive → sessions are opened and closed automatically → completed 
cycles are logged → the stress score contributes to the next health estimate. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ Readings are missing for part of a session → the session is closed with a data-gap flag rather 
than reporting an understated energy figure. 
○ The pack oscillates rapidly around zero current → the settling period prevents a burst of 
spurious short sessions. 
● Business rules: BR-13 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Battery Asset Detail, Analytics 
138 
 
 
Figure 3.92 – Battery Asset Detail 
 
Figure 3.93 – Analytics 
3.11.2 Report Energy, Cost and Carbon 
Function trigger: Web → Analytics; Mobile → Dashboard. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Customer, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Show what the installation is actually delivering in money and emissions. 
139 
 
● Data processing: Sessions are rolled up into per-asset and per-site daily summaries of energy 
charged and discharged, cost saved and CO2 avoided, using the regional electricity tariff and grid 
emission factor effective on the day being summarised. Analytics screens read the pre-computed 
summaries rather than the raw reading stream, so they stay responsive as history grows. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: A summary is produced only for days with at least one completed session. 
Customers see only their own assets and sites. 
● Normal case: The user opens Analytics → selects a period → daily energy, saving and avoided 
emissions are shown per asset or per site. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ No tariff is configured for the region → energy figures are still shown and the cost column 
states that no tariff is configured. 
○ The selected period contains no sessions → an empty chart with an explanatory message is 
shown. 
● Business rules: BR-35 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Analytics, Dashboard, Site Detail 
 
Figure 3.94 – Analytics 
140 
 
 
Figure 3.95 – Dashboard 
 
Figure 3.96 – Site Detail 
3.11.3 Monitor Fleet Health and SLA Compliance 
Function trigger: Web → Dashboard and Analytics. 
Function description:  
● Actor: Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Give the people accountable for the service a single view of how it is performing. 
141 
 
● Data processing: Aggregates asset health distribution, open alert volume by severity, ticket load 
per technician and SLA compliance over the selected period. The SLA view distinguishes tickets 
resolved within the deadline, tickets that breached, and tickets currently at risk. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: Managers see the whole fleet; the figures respect the same ownership rules 
as the underlying records. 
● Normal case: The manager opens the dashboard → sees where the risk is concentrated → drills 
from a chart segment into the underlying alert or ticket list. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The period contains no data → the charts render empty with an explanatory message rather 
than failing. 
○ A technician has no assigned tickets → they are still listed with a zero load so capacity is 
visible. 
● Business rules: BR-08, BR-22 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Dashboard, Analytics, SLA Monitor

3.12 File Storage & Attachments 
3.12.1 Upload and Download Attachments 
Function trigger: Any screen offering an attachment control: ticket, chat, incident, maintenance log, 
article. 
Function description:  
143 
 
● Actor: Customer, Staff, Manager, Admin 
● Purpose: Let evidence travel with the record it belongs to. 
● Data processing: Files are stored in S3-compatible object storage (MinIO) with a recorded 
purpose and status. Upload validates the size and type, and the object is scanned for malware 
before it is committed; a file that fails the scan is deleted from storage and never becomes 
attachable. Download is served through a short-lived signed reference rather than a public URL, 
and every access is recorded with the actor, the file purpose and the outcome. 
Function detail:  
● Data & Validation: The file must be within the configured size limit and of a permitted type. A 
user may only reach files attached to records they are allowed to see. 
● Normal case: The user selects a file → it is validated and stored → the attachment appears on 
the record → another participant downloads it through a signed reference and the access is 
logged. 
● Abnormal case: 
○ The file exceeds the limit → MSG-ERR-17 is shown and nothing is stored. 
○ The type is not permitted → MSG-ERR-18 is shown. 
○ A user requests a file attached to a record they cannot see → MSG-ERR-08 is returned and 
the refusal is logged. 
○ The upload is interrupted → the partial object is discarded and the attachment does not 
appear. 
● Business rules: BR-08, BR-36 (see section 5.1). 
● Screen layout: Ticket Detail, Chat, Environmental Incidents, Maintenance History 
 
Figure 3.100 – Ticket Detail 
144 
 
 
Figure 3.101 – Chat 
 
Figure 3.102 – Environmental Incidents
